### POST-G6 REQUIREMENT-LEVEL TRACEABILITY REOPENING — G-F09

**Audit baseline:** `5bc531aaae2faaf82b596a605e17267d25b4d0b2`

**Defect:** the R2 package contains 112 stable P3 requirements plus binding non-stable/package-local functional consequences, but the canonical DESIGN and PLAN artifacts compressed the final handoff primarily into concern/finding-level summaries. The campaign pair audits carried detailed closure evidence, yet the implementation-governing artifacts did not themselves expose one explicit requirement -> technical solution -> implementation/no-code -> verification chain for every functional obligation. This is insufficient for the zero-material-inference implementation handoff standard.

**Authority consequence at discovery:** R2/G3 remained current because no functional requirement changed. G4-G6 were reopened. Fresh requirement-level DESIGN, compiled modular PLAN revalidation and a new adversarial R3 have now completed; current gate state remains owned only by §18.

**Remediation shape:**
- `solution-design.md` gains an exhaustive 112/112 stable-P3 technical-disposition ledger plus separate package-local/non-stable R2 consequences;
- `implementation-plan.md` remains the deterministic root/task carrier, lists the exact Git blob SHA for each modular authoring source, and compiles the complete phase/action/verification content into its own signed bytes;
- phase files 01-11 own exhaustive requirement-to-solution-to-action traceability for A01-A11;
- `12-action-catalog.md` owns exact file/symbol responsibility, required mutation, prohibited alternatives and proof for IA01-IA24;
- `13-verification-closeout.md` owns checkpoint/test/gate allocation without creating a second validation model;
- fresh DESIGN-A01-A11/H3 and PLAN-A01-A11/H4 must be recorded against the regenerated exact blobs before G4/G5 can pass again.

### POST-G6 SEMANTIC TRACEABILITY REOPENING — G-F10

**Audit baseline:** `e491bcf92fab8fd444822241971e2166d09a6166`

**Finding:** G-F09 closed cardinality and packaging structure but did not establish semantic requirement-level correctness. A detail audit of the exact canonical requirement text, R1 obligation verdicts/evidence, regenerated DESIGN rows, phase files, IA catalog, R3 and permanent fitness sensor found four material defects:

1. **False delta/no-change classification.** Several stable `REFERENCE` requirements were associated with a concern-level finding that addresses a different obligation. Examples include `GW-FR-087-WORKITEM-AUTHORING-OWNERSHIP`, `GW-FR-204-EXACT-HUMAN-INPUT-OWNERSHIP`, `GW-FR-096-LIFECYCLE-AUTHORITY-DISPOSITION`, `GW-FR-225-RESPONSIVE-SEMANTIC-CONTINUITY`, `GW-FR-095-CENTRALIZED-APPLICATION-SETTINGS`, `GW-FR-062/063/071` and `GW-FR-100`, while `GW-FR-012-CATALOG-DRIVEN-AUTHORING` was left as no-code despite the C02 Concerns-language violation.
2. **Unsupported positive no-change closure.** 85/112 stable rows terminate in generic `NO_CODE / IA24 verification`. The campaign DESIGN rule requires requirement-specific positive proof of canonical technical owner/boundary, producer->carrier->consumer path, current realization, consequences, failure/recovery and permanent verification ownership. Generic final semantic review is not that proof.
3. **Concern-compressed package-local trace.** The 22 package-local rows summarize concern acceptance but do not decompose every material required-observable-outcome sub-obligation (for example C18 responsive/keyboard/focus/dirty/modal semantics) into changed versus positively preserved technical realizations.
4. **Mechanical enforcement gap.** `tests/architecture.test.ts` contains no current sensor for the modular PLAN source manifest, 112 requirement rows, SD/IA reference integrity or requirement-specific no-code proof. Green CI therefore cannot mechanically prove G-F09 semantic traceability.

**Gate consequence at discovery:** G2-G6 were reopened because stable-requirement factual evidence and downstream semantic traceability were insufficient. The complete remediation is now recorded in the current R1/R2/DESIGN/PLAN audits and §18; the current R3 is `READY_FOR_SIGNOFF`. This historical discovery statement does not own current gate state.

**Required remediation:**
- first, rebuild R1 evidence for every one of the 112 stable requirements: exact canonical requirement, exact R1 obligation/direct realization evidence, producer->carrier->consumer path, current realization verdict and concrete evidence location; any previously unproven requirement is inspected directly rather than inferred from concern closure;
- revalidate R2 against that factual ledger without changing functional meaning unless the evidence exposes a genuine requirement defect;
- for every one of the 112 stable requirements, record the exact DESIGN realization and classify `TECHNICAL_DELTA | VERIFICATION_DELTA | POSITIVE_NO_CHANGE | DEPENDENCY_DELTA` from exact R1 evidence rather than concern membership;
- for every `POSITIVE_NO_CHANGE`, record requirement-specific positive realization and exact verification owner/proof; no generic `IA24` substitute;
- decompose every material package-local R2 consequence into atomic sub-obligations where one concern paragraph contains multiple independently changed/preserved behaviors;
- map each actual delta to exact SD decision and exact IA action(s); map every IA action back to the exact violated functional obligation/finding;
- distinguish execution owner from consumer/verification dependency when one IA is referenced across phases;
- add permanent architecture/certification fitness for the requirement->SD->IA/no-change structure without creating a second semantic authority;
- rerun DESIGN-A01-A11/H3, PLAN-A01-A11/H4 and a fresh R3 only after the corrected matrices pass.

### G-F10 PERMANENT TRACEABILITY FITNESS

**Verification material:** `tests/architecture.test.ts` blob `dc85a9fb39c2520384448b9348daa932186b449e`, commit `75df8a71c11366ba155b257969709ec969e9f880`.

The permanent architecture gate now structurally enforces the semantic package shape: exact 112 R1/R2/DESIGN/PLAN stable sets, exact evidence/disposition linkage, 24 package atoms, explicit A01-NC01-NC05 corrected-authority trace, IA01-IA25 catalog, exact modular source Git blobs, exact compiled root content, one `order-tasks` block, current task-surface/gate structure, the single G0-G5/G5A/G6 gate progression, and ALIGN-A01-A11/H1 status/record/Q1-Q12 PASS structure. It remains structural and does not infer semantic correctness.

This fitness rule is deliberately structural. It does not infer product semantics or decide which finding belongs to which requirement; those remain governed by R1/R2/DESIGN pair audits and R3. It prevents future count/hash/reference drift from silently invalidating those audits.

## 19. Completion definition

The 0.6.0 design campaign is complete only when all of the following are true:

1. G0-G5, G5A and G6 are PASS.
2. Every registered concern is `CLOSED` or explicitly marked not applicable with evidence.
3. No concern's **Overall** current state is `IN_REVIEW`, `BLOCKED`, `REVALIDATION`, `GAP_FOUND`, or carrying unresolved incoming findings; an R1 phase cell may remain `GAP_FOUND` as the closed factual record of a gap that was subsequently disposed.
4. Every R1 finding has a final disposition.
5. Every R2 requirement has one canonical owner and acceptance criteria.
6. Every applicable work-kind/workflow-step/information/interaction obligation has a complete explicit functional description in R2 and a complete explicit technical realization in DESIGN under §4.3; references/no-change/certification rows alone are insufficient.
7. Every functional obligation has a complete technical realization.
8. Every technical/design obligation has exact P7 implementation or evidence-backed no-code coverage.
9. Every P7 action has upstream functional/design justification.
10. Every mandatory pair audit for R1, R2, DESIGN and PLAN is `PASS`.
11. ALIGN-A01 through ALIGN-A11 are `PASS` against the exact current R2/DESIGN/PLAN package.
12. ALIGN-H1 is `PASS` and proves integrated cross-domain executability and proof ownership.
13. The complete functional -> technical -> implementation -> proof trace is bidirectionally closed with no material inference delegated to implementation.
14. The final horizontal audits pass.
15. R3, run only after G5A PASS, returns `READY_FOR_SIGNOFF`.

The campaign then hands one exact, traceable package to explicit sign-off. It does not authorize implementation by status assertion alone.

- `GW-FR-002-REPRODUCIBLE-AUTHORITY`

**Upstream intent:** Order must turn work into an exact governed package and verified result without letting agent execution, UI state or implementation inference become alternate authority.

**Known downstream consumers:** C02-C20 and C22. C21 owns product-language/identity consequences separately and must remain consistent with C01.

2. Are all lifecycle stages, legal returns, terminal outcomes and sign-off/execution boundaries complete at the functional level?
3. Do current Workbench/state requirements introduce behavior that contradicts or silently extends the lifecycle owner?

5. Which C02-C22 concerns depend on any C01 finding?



### CONCERN END: C01 — Product operating model, scope, success criterion and lifecycle semantics

**End baseline:** `84c882473d202ff3866e6a0dd11c3a1df2fbff4d`  
**R1 verdict:** `GAP_FOUND`

**Authority facts / decision inputs established:**

- `GW-DOC-WORK-LIFECYCLE` owns the canonical WorkItem transition relation; lower layers must defer to it.
- The canonical WorkItem lifecycle is IDEA → R1 → R2 → DESIGN → PLAN → R3 → AWAITING_SIGNOFF → EXECUTION → COMPLETE, with explicit upstream returns and blockers.
- Investigation/Audit are WorkItem kinds with valid R1-terminal paths; they are not additional lifecycle stages.
- Post-signoff validation remains nested inside EXECUTION and does not create new WorkItem stages.
- Exact sign-off is the execution-authority boundary; COMPLETE is terminal.
- The only R2 inputs from this concern are the two bounded taxonomy/presentation ambiguities recorded below.
**Findings:**

- **C01-F01 — P2 lifecycle diagram conflates abstraction levels.** `GW-DOC-SYSTEM-BRIEF §6` visually presents IDEA/R1/R2/DESIGN/PLAN/R3/AWAITING SIGN-OFF together with TODO/IMPLEMENT/MECHANICAL VERIFY/INDEPENDENT REVIEW/CANDIDATE DONE as one linear “Desired lifecycle”. P3 correctly models the latter as post-signoff execution/validation internals under EXECUTION. This is not a state contradiction, but P2 does not explicitly state that the diagram changes abstraction level after sign-off.
- **C01-F02 — “stage” terminology is overloaded in lifecycle context.** `GW-DOC-LIFECYCLE-CONTEXT §6` is titled “Stage decision boundaries” and includes canonical WorkItem stages alongside IMPLEMENT, TEST / MECHANICAL VALIDATION, INDEPENDENT REVIEW and AUDIT. `GW-DOC-WORK-LIFECYCLE` defines the canonical WorkItem stage set differently, and Audit is a WorkItem kind with an R1 terminal path. The intended authority ownership is recoverable from the documents, but the vocabulary permits unnecessary inference.

**Cross-concern findings emitted:**

- **X-C01-C22-01 → C22:** project authority/context resolution must use an explicit taxonomy that distinguishes canonical WorkItem lifecycle stage, WorkItem kind, execution role and validation boundary. C22 cannot close while this distinction remains ambiguous.

**Incoming findings resolved:** none.

**Canonical owners implicated:**

- `GW-DOC-SYSTEM-BRIEF`
- `GW-DOC-WORK-LIFECYCLE`
- `GW-DOC-LIFECYCLE-CONTEXT`
- `GW-DOC-WORKBENCH-UX`
- `GW-DOC-VALIDATION-STANDARD-V1`

**Open product decisions for R2:**

1. minimum amendment to P2's desired-lifecycle presentation so canonical WorkItem stages and nested execution/validation flow are explicit;
2. exact closed terminology for WorkItem stage vs WorkItem kind vs execution role vs validation boundary, propagated to lifecycle-context consumers.

**Open factual questions:** NONE.

**R1 disposition:** `GAP_FOUND` — facts are closed; two bounded documentation/functional-taxonomy gaps require R2 disposition.

**Revalidation trigger:** any change to the canonical WorkItem transition relation, product success criterion, execution validation topology, or lifecycle-context decision-boundary taxonomy.

### CONCERN START: C21 — Product identity, product language, claim boundaries, naming, brand/family positioning

**Start baseline:** `84c882473d202ff3866e6a0dd11c3a1df2fbff4d`  
**Campaign phase:** R1 factual investigation

**Canonical owners:**
- `GW-DOC-PRODUCT-IDENTITY-V1`
- `GW-DOC-SYSTEM-BRIEF`

**Primary authority areas:**
- canonical product name/category/proposition;
- user-level value and differentiation;
- approved product-language hierarchy;
- supporting messages;
- claim boundaries;
- visual identity;
- Pontivus/Span family relationship;
- positioning/naming boundaries.

**Upstream intent:** product language must accurately describe the governed software-work control model established by C01 without inventing capabilities or exposing implementation terminology as product meaning.

**Known downstream consumers:** C01, C05, C19 and public/documentation surfaces.

**Incoming cross-concern findings:** none at start.

**In scope:**
- factual consistency of identity/positioning claims with canonical product authority;
- whether product language still describes the actual governed lifecycle and current capabilities;
- whether claims overstate autonomy, verification, review, delivery or multi-agent behavior;
- consistency between product identity and System Brief.

**Explicitly out of scope:**
- redesigning the visual system (C19);
- changing lifecycle behavior (C01/R2);
- implementation mechanics.

**Questions to close:**
1. Is every approved product claim supported by current P2/P3 behavior?
2. Do naming/category/value statements conflict with the canonical product boundary or non-goals?
3. Does “verified”, “independent”, “software factory”, or agent-related wording imply actors/capabilities the product does not actually guarantee?
4. Is the Order/Span family boundary clear and non-authoritative where required?
5. What product-language decisions, if any, must R2 carry forward?



### CONCERN END: C21 — Product identity, product language, claim boundaries, naming, brand/family positioning

**End baseline:** `84c882473d202ff3866e6a0dd11c3a1df2fbff4d`  
**R1 verdict:** `GAP_FOUND`

**Authority facts / decision inputs established:**

- `GW-DOC-PRODUCT-IDENTITY` bounds Order as a governed software-work control product, not an autonomous product manager, coding agent, CI/CD replacement, deployment authority or merge authority.
- Product intent remains human/project authority: Order governs how authorized work is carried through and must not invent missing product intent.
- Quantified outcome claims require external evidence and are otherwise prohibited.
- Span may supply context/tooling but owns none of Order's lifecycle, approval, execution or verification semantics.
- The R2 inputs are limited to terminal-result terminology and reusable product wording that currently permits over-broad interpretation.
**Findings:**

- **C21-F01 — terminal product outcome is too narrowly named.** Product identity §1 declares **Verified candidate** as the singular “Terminal product outcome”, while §2/§6 and canonical lifecycle authority explicitly admit valid terminal Investigation/Audit complete, disproven, duplicate and already-satisfied outcomes with no candidate. The broader proposition “verified result” can cover both, but the identity table currently collapses the umbrella terminal result into the delivery-specific candidate outcome.
- **C21-F02 — some expanded product wording overstates Order's decision role / delivery path.** The expanded description says “Order works out what needs to happen”, while lifecycle authority says missing P2 intent must block rather than be invented. The standard description also reads as though all work passes through implementation/testing/review, despite explicit no-code terminal behavior elsewhere. The surrounding document corrects both implications, but the canonical reusable phrases themselves are ambiguous.

**Cross-concern findings emitted:**

- **X-C21-C01-01 → C01/R2:** functional/product wording must preserve the distinction between a general verified terminal result and a delivery-specific verified candidate, and must not imply that Order originates missing product intent. C01 R1 facts already establish these semantics, so no factual re-open is required.

**Incoming findings resolved:** none.

**Canonical owners implicated:**
- `GW-DOC-PRODUCT-IDENTITY`
- `GW-DOC-SYSTEM-BRIEF`
- `GW-DOC-WORK-LIFECYCLE`

**Open product decisions for R2:**

1. exact canonical terminology for umbrella terminal **result** versus implementation **candidate**;
2. minimum changes to reusable product-language hierarchy so it describes definition/execution “as applicable” and does not imply autonomous product-intent invention.

**Open factual questions:** NONE.

**R1 disposition:** `GAP_FOUND`.

**Revalidation trigger:** any change to product outcome taxonomy, P2 product boundary, or the approved product-language hierarchy.

| C21-O06 | Coding agents are execution components, not product authority; builder cannot redefine/certify work | Product Identity §§3-6 -> signed task/provider roles -> validation/reviewer flow -> README supporting copy | CONFORMS |

| C21-O11 | Public reusable descriptions must preserve conditional implementation and verified-result/candidate distinction | Product Identity §5 -> root README hero/intro/how-it-works copy | VIOLATES — C21-F03 |

**Information hierarchy:** product-facing primary language remains brand proposition + verified-result abstraction; technical execution language is subordinate. Application workflow copy uses canonical Workbench names and terminal-result copy distinguishes candidate versus non-candidate outcomes.

### CONCERN END: C02 — WorkItem intake, kinds, creation and metadata ownership

**End baseline:** `84c882473d202ff3866e6a0dd11c3a1df2fbff4d`  
**R1 verdict:** `GAP_FOUND`

**Authority facts / decision inputs established:**

- `GW-DOC-WORK-ITEM-DOCUMENT-SYSTEM` owns WorkItem schema/kind, portable source identity/classification, authoring metadata ownership and linked-remediation lineage.
- WorkItem kind is derived from the pinned schema; ordinary authoring must not create duplicate mutable kind/lifecycle/role/gate owners.
- The portable Work request remains the canonical originating intent source; runtime persistence is projection only.
- Linked remediation is same-project, bounded to the permitted parent/child kinds and immutable lineage semantics.
- R2 decisions are limited to ordinary concern presentation and same-project linked-remediation authoring; C02-F03 is a DESIGN ownership issue, not a product fallback requirement.
**Findings:**

- **C02-F01 — technical identifier leakage in ordinary Create.** `CreateWorkItem.tsx` renders the ordinary field label **Concern IDs** and raw identifier options. Functional authoring calls this **Concerns**, and Workbench information hierarchy classifies raw IDs as technical information. Selection is catalog-controlled rather than free text, but the presentation still exposes implementation identity as ordinary product language.
- **C02-F02 — linked remediation exposes an invalid Project choice.** The linked-remediation flow initializes the parent project but renders the same editable Project Selector. Canonical lineage is same-project only; changing Project can never succeed and is rejected only by the daemon. The browser therefore exposes an action space containing a known-invalid lineage choice.
- **C02-F03 — productive ingestion ownership is not singular.** Canonical source-consumer closure requires all authored WorkItems to enter through one productive ingestion/reconciliation owner. Implementation evidence proves that obligation is not yet realized. DESIGN must consolidate ownership without inventing a second representation.

**Cross-concern findings emitted:**

- **X-C02-C04-01 → C04:** the source/reconciliation investigation must determine the single productive ingestion owner and eliminate duplicated UI-create registration semantics without creating a new source authority.

**Incoming findings resolved:** none.

**Open product decisions for R2:**

- C02-F01/F02 require no new domain model; R2 should preserve existing canonical semantics and make the ordinary interaction unambiguous.
- C02-F03 is primarily a DESIGN/implementation ownership issue; R2 should reference the existing source-backed creation requirement rather than invent another behavior.

**Open factual questions:** NONE.

**R1 disposition:** `GAP_FOUND`.

**Revalidation trigger:** changes to schema catalog ownership/defaults, creation contract, WorkItem metadata authority, or remediation lineage.

4. Are archive/restore and project filters consistent with the canonical source/project boundary?
5. Can stale or invalid source state be hidden by queue projection/search?
6. What queue/discovery gaps require R2 or DESIGN decisions?



### CONCERN END: C03 — Queue, repository discovery/reconciliation, active/archive visibility and work selection

**End baseline:** `84c882473d202ff3866e6a0dd11c3a1df2fbff4d`  
**R1 verdict:** `GAP_FOUND`

**Authority facts / decision inputs established:**

- Queue/discovery behavior is governed by portable-source reconciliation and repository-native archive semantics, not by a second installation-local work registry.
- Discovery/reconciliation must deterministically classify valid, invalid, missing, duplicate, misplaced and changed sources without fabricating lifecycle/run/approval history.
- Archive is separate from lifecycle state and must preserve WorkItem identity/history.
- Productive boundaries must observe the authoritative reconciled source rather than a stale projection.
- The only R2 gap is C03-F01: active filters/search must remain semantically consistent when archived work is included.
**Finding:**

- **C03-F01 — archived inclusion violates canonical queue-filter semantics.** The authority requires archive to alter membership visibility without creating a second queue/filter meaning. Implementation evidence proves project/search filtering is not preserved when archived work is included. R2 must close the required semantic outcome; incidental UI composition is not a design baseline.

**Cross-concern findings emitted:** none.

**Incoming findings resolved:** none.

**Open product decisions for R2:** preserve one queue filtering/search semantics when archived items are included. This does not require new persistent state.

**Open factual questions:** NONE.

**R1 disposition:** `GAP_FOUND`.

**Revalidation trigger:** changes to queue visibility ownership, repository configuration/disconnect semantics, reconciliation dispositions or archive projection.

### CONCERN END: C04 — WorkItem source, title, portable metadata, authoring, document Git and repository durability

**End baseline:** `84c882473d202ff3866e6a0dd11c3a1df2fbff4d`  
**R1 verdict:** `GAP_FOUND`

**Authority facts / decision inputs established:**

- The Work request file is the single portable source owner for originating intent, metadata and title; editing must not create parallel hidden authority.
- Source and lifecycle-document mutations are freshness/hash protected and must route through their canonical owning stage/authority reactions.
- Rediscovered lifecycle Markdown is documentary material only; file existence cannot fabricate workflow history or conformance.
- Documentation Git persistence is an explicit operator action over the governed WorkItem document set, separate from editor save.
- C04-F01 is a DESIGN ownership defect: UI creation and repository reconciliation must converge on one existing source-admission/reconciliation owner, without a new model or DTO.
**Finding:**

- **C04-F01 — productive source admission lacks one canonical owner.** Source-consumer closure requires one ingestion/reconciliation owner for all source-backed WorkItems. Implementation evidence confirms the ownership defect identified by C02-F03 / X-C02-C04-01. DESIGN must consolidate around the existing canonical owner, not preserve either implementation path by default.

**Cross-concern findings emitted:** none beyond the DESIGN consequence already owned by C04-F01.

**Incoming findings resolved:**
- **X-C02-C04-01:** confirmed and consolidated into C04-F01.

**Open product decisions for R2:** NONE. Existing canonical source-authoring, freshness and documentation-Git authority is sufficient; no additional R2 product delta is required.

**Open technical decision for DESIGN:** consolidate initial source registration through the same canonical reconciliation/admission path used for repository-authored WorkItems while preserving exact creation semantics.

**Open factual questions:** NONE.

**R1 disposition:** `GAP_FOUND`.

**Revalidation trigger:** changes to source parser/writer ownership, source mutation admission, lifecycle-artifact edit semantics, or documentation Git ownership.

### G0 downstream revalidation note — v2 R1 template authority lineage

C06 discovered that current `change-v2` and `defect-v2` WorkItems were routed to historical `change-r1-v1` / `defect-r1-v1` templates. Because `stagePrompt()` injects the template verbatim as the **Canonical stage template**, current v2 Research received a historical `GW-DOC-LIFECYCLE-CONTEXT-V1` authority pin alongside current resolved authority.

This is a Phase-0-class lineage defect, not a C06 product-design decision.

The first correction exposed the same failure class for ordinary Investigation/Audit authoring: both kinds were still defaulting to historical `investigation-v1` / `audit-v1` pins. Phase 0 therefore also adds current `investigation-v2` / `audit-v2` defaults with unchanged intake/R1 structure and terminal outcomes, current lifecycle-context V2 authority, and no downstream delivery stages.

The Phase 0 package now:
- retains v1 R1 templates unchanged for v1-pinned WorkItems;
- adds `change-r1-v2` / `defect-r1-v2` with the same evidence structure and `GW-DOC-LIFECYCLE-CONTEXT-V2`;
- routes only current delivery v2 schemas to those current R1 templates;
- adds a permanent core regression test;
- documents the rule in `GW-DOC-WORK-ITEM-DOCUMENT-SYSTEM-V2` and the Phase 0 WorkItem.

**Corrected Phase 0 candidate:** `3c02aaf7d76c3d58967845ee475bc4c605dd347f`.

At this historical checkpoint, the then-relevant G0 authority correction was verified on `006477f84b0290803b712fdef96886172ea7b14e`. C01-C05 facts remained valid and C06 was rechecked and closed. Later Phase 0 corrections advanced the campaign's current G0 factual baseline to `732cfb66ab1b71ad1454b69e668cb7641e140988`; this checkpoint does not override the current G0 record in §5.

### G0 downstream revalidation note — v2 DESIGN template completeness

C08 identified a second downstream validation defect in the Phase 0 authoring foundation. The current Change/Defect v2 DESIGN templates were structurally narrower than the binding `GW-DOC-SPECIFICATION-STANDARD` S7 contract: they did not explicitly require existing-capability/reuse analysis, behavior to replace/delete, necessary-new-concept justification or rejected alternatives/rationale.

Because these schema templates are injected into productive DESIGN prompts and define the required document sections, the omission is a foundational authoring-contract defect rather than a 0.6.0 product-design decision.

The Phase 0 correction:
- extends both current v2 DESIGN templates to carry the complete S7 content contract;
- preserves historical v1 design templates unchanged;
- aligns the WorkItem-document and canonical-contract owners;
- adds permanent regression coverage.

**Corrected Phase 0 candidate:** `d066b3b6fc1347909d11ea5aa376014801081fd5`.

C01-C07 facts remain valid. The DESIGN-template correction is verified by the campaign-relevant test suite on `732cfb66`; C08 remains open and resumes from its existing START record.


### G0 downstream revalidation note — mechanical readiness timing

The foundation audit identified that complete signed-task mechanical validation was performed only by the Approval/sign-off package path after R3 had already returned `READY_FOR_SIGNOFF`. This contradicted the binding requirement that mechanically checkable readiness be closed before readiness promotion.

The Phase 0 correction reuses the existing `validateTaskDefinitions` owner at the R3 coordinator boundary only for `READY_FOR_SIGNOFF`, using the exact current PLAN and current material closure. Invalid gates, concerns, authority/acceptance refs, scope/exclusions or signed-material change surfaces now block R3 conformance. Return-to-* outcomes remain available so R3 can route a defective package upstream rather than being trapped by the defect it is reporting.

A daemon regression exercises the real provider→R3 completion path.

**Corrected Phase 0 candidate:** `732cfb66ab1b71ad1454b69e668cb7641e140988`.

### C09 foundational finding — G-F04 current-v2 PLAN authoring-contract completeness

Canonical authority is unambiguous:

- `GW-DOC-SPECIFICATION-STANDARD` S8 requires each implementation-plan task to identify objective, requirement/design traceability, expected production surface, module ownership/decomposition constraints, expected test/validation surface, required deletions/replacements, dependencies and completion evidence, including architecture-feedback checkpoints where a task contains ordered architecture-affecting steps.
- S9 requires an explicit minimum-change forecast covering production, test/gate and documentation/contract surfaces plus any expected new state/schema/dependency/configuration/migration/abstraction/API.
- S10 requires the logical verification contract proving required behavior, prior required behavior, architecture/governance, authorized scope and minimum sufficiency.
- `GW-DOC-LIFECYCLE-CONTEXT` requires PLAN to produce the P7 implementation plan, explicit execution-task definitions, change-surface forecast and verification contract without inventing new P1-P6 decisions.

The current v2 PLAN templates do not bind that complete content contract. `change-plan-v2` requires only exact task units/dependencies, traceability, expected change surface, deletions/additions/modifications, validation sequence and the canonical `order-tasks` block. `defect-plan-v2` substitutes root-cause/remediation and regression/preservation wording but is similarly narrower than S8-S10. The structural admission check proves only those reduced template headings, while PLAN promotion mechanically parses the `order-tasks` block. The canonical block is correctly singular and must remain the execution-task representation, but it does not by itself replace the surrounding S8-S10 plan obligations.

This is the same failure class previously exposed by G-F02 at DESIGN: productive authoring can omit content that later readiness authority expects. It is therefore a foundation defect, not a C09 product-design delta and not a reason to preserve or redesign the current UI.

**Required remediation boundary:**
- extend only the current Change/Defect v2 PLAN authoring/template contract so it explicitly carries the complete S8-S10 obligations;
- preserve all historical v1 PLAN templates/pins unchanged;
- keep the single canonical `order-tasks` representation; do not introduce another task DTO/model/block;
- do not move P1-P6 decisions into PLAN;
- align the current template owner/P6 documentation as required;
- strengthen PLAN conformance questions/admission only to the extent needed to prevent structurally incomplete S8-S10 output from promoting;
- add permanent regression coverage proving current-v2 PLAN completeness and historical/current separation.

**C09 historical R1 disposition:** `BLOCKED` until G-F04 remediation; resolved below on exact candidate `e91d3e5`.

**Closed C01-C08 impact:** none established. G-F04 concerns PLAN authoring/conformance; it does not change the P2/P3 authority or factual conclusions on which C01-C08 closed.

### CONCERN END: C10 — R3 readiness review, adversarial challenge and return routing

**End baseline:** `e91d3e5e3d0164a79f657e0cff02fdc0ba46a096`  
**R1 verdict:** `VERIFIED_WITH_FINDING`.

**Authority facts / decision inputs established:**
- R3 receives the complete relevant P1-P7 authority chain, applicable P0 controls, current repository evidence needed for freshness/feasibility, and earlier conformance evidence. It is an independent adversarial judgment and must treat R1/R2/DESIGN/PLAN as untrusted.
- R3 may decide only readiness and the exact defective owning layer; it may not repair requirements, design or plan on behalf of those stages.
- `READY_FOR_SIGNOFF` requires coherent/current/traceable authority, complete current lifecycle material, bidirectional traceability, canonical-owner uniqueness, zero unresolved material authority conflicts/decisions and evidence supporting the verdict.
- Complete mechanically resolvable signed-task validation now executes against the exact current PLAN/material closure before `READY_FOR_SIGNOFF` promotion. The validation is deliberately conditional on that outcome so `RETURN_TO_*` remains available to route a defective package upstream.
- Canonical R3 completion outcomes route deterministically: `RETURN_TO_RESEARCH -> R1`, `RETURN_TO_REQUIREMENTS -> R2`, `RETURN_TO_DESIGN -> DESIGN`, `RETURN_TO_PLAN -> PLAN`; `READY_FOR_SIGNOFF` alone advances to Approval eligibility. Blocking remains an operational state of the owning stage.
- Readiness Review is canonically Review-class and read-only. The required primary hierarchy is verdict/current readiness state, findings/defective owner, package material, evidence, then Run history, except when actual required input temporarily owns attention.

**Finding C10-F01 — Readiness verdict presentation projects the wrong semantic state.**

Canonical authority `GW-FR-221-READINESS-REVIEW-HIERARCHY` and Workbench §14.6 require the readiness verdict to dominate the Readiness Review. Implementation evidence in `ui/src/ReadinessWorkspace.tsx` shows the section labelled **Readiness verdict** renders `materialPresentationStateLabel(materialState)` (for example Current/Stale), which is document maturity/currentness rather than the canonical R3 readiness outcome. The governed R3 document is rendered below in the findings material, so the actual `READY_FOR_SIGNOFF | RETURN_TO_* | BLOCKED` judgment is not projected into the primary verdict slot.

This is an existing-authority implementation violation. It does not define current UI as a baseline and does not create a new product requirement/state model.

**Required R2 disposition:** `REFERENCE` — `GW-FR-221-READINESS-REVIEW-HIERARCHY` applies unchanged. The reconstructed functional design must expose the canonical readiness verdict as the primary readiness state and must not substitute material maturity for that verdict.

**Cross-concern findings emitted:** C10-F01 may affect C17 current-attention/recovery presentation only if later investigation proves the same semantic substitution outside Readiness; no such cross-concern fact is asserted here.

**Open product decisions for R2:** NONE.

**Open factual questions:** NONE.

**R1 disposition:** `FACTS_CLOSED`.

**Revalidation trigger:** changes to R3 outcome semantics, readiness mechanical-validation timing, exact return routing, or `GW-FR-221` Readiness Review hierarchy.

### CONCERN END: C11 — Approval/sign-off, exact-package identity, staleness and upstream recovery

**End baseline:** `8b598efd2270f11c2832f6255acbd75f210f49f2`  
**R1 verdict:** `VERIFIED_WITH_FINDING`.

**Authority facts / decision inputs established:**
- Only `R3 / READY_FOR_SIGNOFF` with current conformance may enter Approval; reaching Approval is not approval and execution authority exists only after explicit sign-off of the exact current package.
- The package binds the exact WorkItem source snapshot, baseline commit, signed PLAN task block, R3 root and the persisted transitive material dependency closure. P6 keeps one canonical signed-task representation rather than duplicating upstream authority into Approval fields.
- Sign-off re-resolves the exact package, checks the expected package hash and final repository baseline, verifies current source/material bytes, then atomically persists Approval, materialized execution tasks and the lifecycle transition.
- Material source/governance/verification change invalidates Approval. Stale Approval is not signable and reconciliation returns ownership to the earliest canonical changed-material owner rather than silently refreshing or locally repairing the package.
- Approval is canonically one Review-class workspace. Package meaning/currentness/readiness and signed task scope/gates dominate hashes/commit identity; explicit approver identity and sign-off remain adjacent to the exact judged package.

**Finding C11-F01 — Approval overview does not surface readiness as primary review state.**

`GW-DOC-WORKBENCH-UX` §14.7 requires the primary Approval hierarchy to begin with package currentness **and readiness**, followed by signed task meaning/scope/gates. Implementation evidence in `ui/src/SignoffReview.tsx` presents package currentness and task scope correctly, but it does not project the canonical R3 readiness judgment into the primary Approval overview; R3 is only indirectly available as governed package material.

This is an existing-authority presentation violation. It does not change sign-off semantics, package identity, task ownership or stale-authority routing.

**Required R2 disposition:** `REFERENCE` — existing Approval hierarchy authority applies unchanged. The reconstructed functional design must make exact package readiness/currentness directly legible in the primary Approval judgment surface without promoting hashes/ids or creating another readiness state.

**Cross-concern findings emitted:** NONE. C10-F01 and C11-F01 are separate projections of the same canonical R3 outcome into their respective Review surfaces and do not require reopening C10 facts.

**Open product decisions for R2:** NONE.

**Open factual questions:** NONE.

**R1 disposition:** `GAP_FOUND`.

**Revalidation trigger:** changes to sign-off package identity/freshness, explicit authorization semantics, stale-authority routing, or Approval Review hierarchy.

### CONCERN END: C12 — Execution, task ownership, builder/reviewer/remediator progression and validation

**End baseline:** `64f36a85c4a8f554a40388d1a306d38b9f29beaa`  
**R1 verdict:** `VERIFIED`.

**Authority facts / decision inputs established:**
- Execution authority is derived only from explicit Approval and canonical signed task definitions; workers cannot create or expand tasks.
- Exactly one current execution owner is derived from current Approval/task/Run state. Manual Start targets that exact owner; the global scheduler remains a separate existing automatic-selection path rather than a second task authority.
- BUILDER, REVIEWER and REMEDIATOR Runs are durable role-specific execution records. Independent review cannot edit the candidate.
- V0-V7 is a real validation control plane. Candidate completion requires current evidence through every required boundary; builder self-attestation cannot substitute for validation.
- Repairable `TEST_FAILURE`, `REVIEW_FAILURE` and authorized `CHANGE_BLOAT` are bounded. Specification, stale approval, scope and governance failures return/block according to their canonical owner rather than being blindly repaired.
- Explicit Resume changes only the blocked current owner back to an eligible state after the blocker is resolved; it does not create a Run or advance lifecycle.
- Implementation is stable master/detail. Historical task/Run selection is inspection state and does not change current task ownership, blocker or primary action.

**Findings:** NONE.

**Open factual questions:** NONE.

**R1 verdict:** `VERIFIED`.

**Authority facts / decision inputs established:**

- Evidence is read-only exact material and remains evidence; it cannot override canonical authority by presentation.
- Material maturity/currentness is a derived product projection over reach, conformance, freshness and signed-lock facts, not another persisted document-state machine.
- `DRAFT` is specifically unreached material. A reached current snapshot without current conformance derives `UPDATED_VALIDATION_REQUIRED`, preserving editability for the owning stage.
- R3 and signed lifecycle material are read-only; production/runtime/evidence state never becomes editable through the Markdown workbench.

### CONCERN END: C15 — OpenCode/provider interaction, session continuity, observable activity, input and failure semantics

**End baseline:** `1387407fa695cf7b47b53e9796c95fd0fd428d3e`  
**R1 verdict:** `VERIFIED`.

**Authority facts / decision inputs established:**
- OpenCode is an execution provider only; it owns no workflow, task, approval, retry, project-context or completion authority.
- Every Order Run begins with a fresh provider session. Same-Run continuation is admitted only while stage/context/model/permissions/workspace identity remain current.
- Replacement sessions are reconstructed from current canonical Order context and persisted evidence; old conversation is evidence, not authority.
- Productive turns use deterministic prompt admission/correlation and Order-controlled completion reconciliation. Provider liveness/event streams are not completion authority.
- Provider-native activity is allowlisted/redacted into bounded coordinator-owned RunEvents. Missing live activity never changes Run correctness.
- Required human input and conversation are durable Order RunEvents. Cancellation and provider failure produce canonical lifecycle/task consequences rather than hidden session behavior.
- Provider-process loss is bounded: pre-signoff may replace from current context; post-signoff follows one bounded operational replacement policy and otherwise blocks.

**Findings:** NONE.

**Cross-concern findings emitted:** NONE.

**Open product decisions for R2:** NONE.

**Open factual questions:** NONE.

**R1 disposition:** `FACTS_CLOSED`.

**Revalidation trigger:** changes to provider/session continuity, prompt admission/completion, activity projection, required input durability, cancellation or provider-failure replacement.

### CONCERN END: C16 — Settings, installation readiness, repositories/document roots, accounts, models and reference roots

**End baseline:** `a186296c2a1657e13fc851dc402a0cc356f1a09e`  
**R1 verdict:** `VERIFIED_WITH_FINDING`.

**Authority facts / decision inputs established:**
- Installation success and productive readiness are distinct; readiness is derived from canonical configuration/integration state and is not another lifecycle state.
- Settings is the single ordinary application-level owner for target repositories, OpenCode accounts and productive model/runtime selection. First-run/recovery routes into that same state rather than creating another configuration model.
- Target repositories are exact validated Git roots. Project-local document roots are repository-owned manifest state; moving an existing populated root is an explicit bounded migration, not a field edit.
- Reference roots are installation configuration for read-only STAGE context and never become target repositories or project authority.
- Saved OpenCode accounts remain OpenCode-owned credential state in Order's private namespace; account/model changes are explicit configuration actions.
- Missing repository/account/model readiness is surfaced before productive work rather than through downstream provider failure.

**Finding C16-F01 — Settings promotes technical filesystem/transport identity into ordinary primary content.**

`GW-DOC-INSTALLATION-READINESS-V1` §§7.2/7.5 require project identity, readiness and relevant actions to dominate connected-repository presentation while exact Git/document paths and diagnostic identifiers remain secondary unless needed to disambiguate. Current `ui/src/SettingsWorkspace.tsx` renders `Git root` and `WorkItem documents` as persistent primary repository-table columns and exposes the raw model-list HTTP source as ordinary supporting content.

This is an existing-authority information-hierarchy violation, not a configuration-contract defect.

**Required R2 disposition:** `REFERENCE` — existing Settings hierarchy applies unchanged. The reconstructed functional design must keep operator decisions/readiness primary and move exact paths/API identity into secondary/on-demand technical detail except where needed for the active configuration task.

### CONCERN END: C17 — Current attention, blocking, errors, stale authority, retry/resume/recovery and asynchronous convergence

**End baseline:** `03bb8d305f9e3d1d7095f46a1de5ca2bff301ce5`

**Authority facts / decision inputs established:**
- Current attention is selected from one closed precedence language in the functional core: convergence, mutation conflict, required input, proposal decision/adoption, blocker, stale authority, recovery, processing, actionable, ordinary.
- Recovery is separately closed to `NONE | RETRY_CURRENT_STAGE | RESUME_CURRENT_OWNER | RECONCILE_AUTHORITY`; stale authority specifically maps to reconciliation rather than generic Resume.
- Observation coherence is evaluated before actions. Canonically inconsistent dependent observations derive `CONVERGING` and suppress workflow mutations until refetch converges.
- Historical material selection does not alter current attention, execution owner or recovery. The shell preserves the current attention signal/action while historical detail remains inspectable.
- Mutation conflicts, proposal ownership and active Runs suppress lower-priority competing mutations.
- C10-F01 is local to Readiness verdict presentation; no broader C17 semantic-state substitution was found.
